<ul><li><span class="nowrap"><span class="noviewer" typeof="mw:File"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Yellow_flag_waving.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="icon" resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Yellow_flag_waving.svg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1b/Yellow_flag_waving.svg/20px-Yellow_flag_waving.svg.png" decoding="async" data-file-width="249" data-file-height="268" data-file-type="drawing" height="15" width="14" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1b/Yellow_flag_waving.svg/40px-Yellow_flag_waving.svg.png 2x" class="mw-file-element"/></a></span> </span><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portal:Liberalism" title="Portal:Liberalism">Liberalism<span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>portal</a></li>
<li><span class="nowrap"><span class="mw-image-border noviewer" typeof="mw:File"><span><img alt="flag" resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Flag_of_France.svg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/c/c3/Flag_of_France.svg/20px-Flag_of_France.svg.png" decoding="async" data-file-width="900" data-file-height="600" data-file-type="drawing" height="11" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portal:France" title="Portal:France">France<span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>portal</a></li></ul></td></tr><tr><td class="sidebar-navbar"><span class="mw-empty-elt"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1333133064" about="#mwt32" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Hlist/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1239400231" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" about="#mwt33" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Navbar/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'>.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:inline;font-size:88%;font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .navbar-collapse{float:left;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .navbar-boxtext{word-spacing:0}.mw-parser-output .navbar ul{display:inline-block;white-space:nowrap;line-height:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::before{margin-right:-0.125em;content:"[ "}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::after{margin-left:-0.125em;content:" ]"}.mw-parser-output .navbar print{.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:none!important}}</style></span><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:EditPage/Template:Liberalism_in_France" title="Special:EditPage/Template:Liberalism in France"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>

<p id="mwDg"><b id="mwDw">Victor-Marie Hugo, vicomte Hugo</b><sup about="#mwt39" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-1"}}'><a href="#cite_note-1" id="mwEA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwEQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwEg">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwEw">]</span></span></a></sup> (<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1177148991" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt34" id="mwFA" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:IPA/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"IPA","href":"./Template:IPA"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"viktɔʁ maʁi yɡo"},"3":{"wt":"lang"},"4":{"wt":"Victor Hugo.ogg"}},"i":0}}]}'>.mw-parser-output .IPA-label-small{font-size:85%}.mw-parser-output .references .IPA-label-small,.mw-parser-output .infobox .IPA-label-small,.mw-parser-output .navbox .IPA-label-small{font-size:100%}</style><span class="IPA-label IPA-label-small" about="#mwt34">French:</span><span about="#mwt34"> </span><span class="IPA nowrap" lang="fr-Latn-fonipa" about="#mwt34"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/French" title="Help:IPA/French"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span>viktɔʁ<span class="wrap"> </span>maʁi<span class="wrap"> </span>yɡo<span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></a></span><span about="#mwt34"> </span><span class="ext-phonos" typeof="mw:Extension/phonos" about="#mwt34" data-mw='{"name":"phonos","attrs":{"file":"Victor Hugo.ogg"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'><span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-buttonElement-size-medium oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui='{"_":"mw.Phonos.PhonosButton","href":"\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/a\/a6\/Victor_Hugo.ogg\/Victor_Hugo.ogg.mp3","rel":["nofollow"],"framed":false,"icon":"volumeUp","data":{"ipa":"","text":"","lang":"en","wikibase":"","file":"Victor Hugo.ogg"},"classes":["noexcerpt","ext-phonos-PhonosButton","ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel"]}'><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/a/a6/Victor_Hugo.ogg/Victor_Hugo.ogg.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" title="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt navigation-not-searchable"><a href="/wiki/File:Victor_Hugo.ogg" title="File:Victor Hugo.ogg">ⓘ</a></sup></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Pages_with_French_IPA" about="#mwt34"/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Pages_including_recorded_pronunciations" about="#mwt34" id="mwFQ"/>; 26 February 1802 – 22 May 1885) was a French <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanticism" title="Romanticism" id="mwFg">Romantic</a> author, poet, essayist, playwright, journalist, human rights activist and politician.</p>

<p id="mwFw">His most famous works are the novels <i id="mwGA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hunchback_of_Notre-Dame" title="The Hunchback of Notre-Dame" id="mwGQ">The Hunchback of Notre-Dame</a></i> (1831) and <i id="mwGg"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_Misérables" title="Les Misérables" id="mwGw">Les Misérables</a></i> (1862). In France, Hugo is renowned for his poetry collections, such as <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt40" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwHA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Les Contemplations]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_Contemplations" title="Les Contemplations">Les Contemplations</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt40" id="mwHQ"/> and <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt41" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwHg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[La Légende des siècles]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Légende_des_siècles" title="La Légende des siècles">La Légende des siècles</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt41" id="mwHw"/> (<i id="mwIA">The Legend of the Ages)</i>. Hugo was at the forefront of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanticism" title="Romanticism" id="mwIQ">Romantic literary movement</a> with his play <i id="mwIg"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cromwell_(play)" title="Cromwell (play)" id="mwIw">Cromwell</a></i> and drama <i id="mwJA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hernani_(drama)" title="Hernani (drama)" id="mwJQ">Hernani</a></i>. His works have inspired music, both during his lifetime and after his death, including the opera <i id="mwJg"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rigoletto" title="Rigoletto" id="mwJw">Rigoletto</a></i> and the musicals <i id="mwKA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_Misérables_(musical)" title="Les Misérables (musical)" id="mwKQ">Les Misérables</a></i> and <i id="mwKg"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Notre-Dame_de_Paris_(musical)" title="Notre-Dame de Paris (musical)" id="mwKw">Notre-Dame de Paris</a></i>. He produced more than 4,000 drawings in his lifetime, and campaigned for social causes such as the abolition of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_punishment_in_France" title="Capital punishment in France" id="mwLA">capital punishment</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abolitionism" title="Abolitionism" id="mwLQ">slavery</a>.</p>

<p id="mwLg">Although he was a committed <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royalist" title="Royalist" id="mwLw">royalist</a> when young, Hugo's views changed as the decades passed, and he became a passionate supporter of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republicanism" title="Republicanism" id="mwMA">republicanism</a>, serving in politics as both deputy and senator. His work touched upon most of the political and social issues and the artistic trends of his time. His opposition to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Absolutism_(European_history)" title="Absolutism (European history)" id="mwMQ">absolutism</a>, and his literary stature, established him as a national hero. Hugo died on 22 May 1885, aged 83. He was given a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_funeral" title="State funeral" id="mwMg">state funeral</a> in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panthéon" title="Panthéon" id="mwMw">Panthéon</a> of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris" title="Paris" id="mwNA">Paris</a>, which was attended by over two million people, the largest in French history.<sup about="#mwt44" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-2"}}'><a href="#cite_note-2" id="mwNQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwNg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwNw">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwOA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" id="mwOQ" data-mw='{"autoGenerated":true}'/></section><section data-mw-section-id="1" id="mwOg" aria-labelledby="Early_life"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Early_life">Early life</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Victor_Hugo&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Early life"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwOw">Victor-Marie Hugo was born on 26 February 1802 (7 <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ventôse" title="Ventôse" id="mwPA">Ventôse</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Republican_calendar" title="French Republican calendar" id="mwPQ">year X of the Republic</a><sup about="#mwt45" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEEscholier1932358_3-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTEEscholier1932358"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEEscholier1932358-3"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Escholier"},"2":{"wt":"1932"},"p":{"wt":"358"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEEscholier1932358-3" id="mwPg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwPw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwQA">[</span>3<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwQQ">]</span></span></a></sup>) in <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt48" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwQg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Besançon]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Besançon" title="Besançon">Besançon</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt48" id="mwQw"/> in Eastern France. He was the youngest son of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Léopold_Sigisbert_Hugo" title="Joseph Léopold Sigisbert Hugo" id="mwRA">Joseph Léopold Sigisbert Hugo</a>, a general in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleonic_army" title="Napoleonic army" class="mw-redirect" id="mwRQ">Napoleonic army</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sophie_Trébuchet" title="Sophie Trébuchet" id="mwRg">Sophie Trébuchet</a>. The couple had two other sons: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abel_Hugo" title="Abel Hugo" id="mwRw">Abel Joseph</a> and <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt49" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwSA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Eugène"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;">Eugène</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt49" id="mwSQ"/>. The Hugo family came from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy,_France" title="Nancy, France" id="mwSg">Nancy</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lorraine" title="Lorraine" id="mwSw">Lorraine</a>, where Hugo's grandfather was a wood merchant. Léopold enlisted in the army of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolutionary_France" title="Revolutionary France" class="mw-redirect" id="mwTA">Revolutionary France</a> at fourteen. He was an atheist and an ardent supporter of the Republic. Hugo's mother Sophie was loyal to the deposed <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_of_Bourbon" title="House of Bourbon" id="mwTQ">dynasty</a> but would declare her children to be Protestants.<sup about="#mwt52" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-4" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-4"}}'><a href="#cite_note-4" id="mwTg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwTw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwUA">[</span>4<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwUQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt55" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-5" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-5"}}'><a href="#cite_note-5" id="mwUg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwUw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwVA">[</span>5<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwVQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt58" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-6" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-6"}}'><a href="#cite_note-6" id="mwVg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwVw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwWA">[</span>6<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwWQ">]</span></span></a></sup> They met in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Châteaubriant" title="Châteaubriant" id="mwWg">Châteaubriant</a> in 1796 and married the following year.<sup about="#mwt61" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Robb_1999_p._7-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Robb 1999 p."},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Robb_1999_p.-7"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Robb_1999_p.-7" id="mwWw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwXA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwXQ">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwXg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwXw">Since Hugo's father was an officer in Napoleon's army, the family moved frequently from posting to posting. Léopold Hugo wrote to his son that he had been conceived on one of the highest peaks in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vosges_Mountains" title="Vosges Mountains" class="mw-redirect" id="mwYA">Vosges Mountains</a>, on a journey from Lunéville to Besançon. "This elevated origin," he went on, "seems to have had effects on you so that your muse is now continually sublime."<sup about="#mwt62" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-8" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-8"}}'><a href="#cite_note-8" id="mwYQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwYg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwYw">[</span>8<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwZA">]</span></span></a></sup> Hugo believed himself to have been conceived on 24 June 1801, which is the origin of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Valjean" title="Jean Valjean" id="mwZQ">Jean Valjean</a>'s prisoner number <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/24601" title="24601" class="mw-redirect" id="mwZg">24601</a>.<sup about="#mwt65" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-9" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-9"}}'><a href="#cite_note-9" id="mwZw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwaA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwaQ">[</span>9<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwag">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwaw">In 1810, Hugo's father was made Count Hugo de Cogolludo y Sigüenza by then King of Spain <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Bonaparte" title="Joseph Bonaparte" id="mwbA">Joseph Bonaparte</a>,<sup about="#mwt68" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-dHauterive_1845_p._223_10-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"dHauterive 1845 p. 223"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-dHauterive_1845_p._223-10"}}'><a href="#cite_note-dHauterive_1845_p._223-10" id="mwbQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwbg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwbw">[</span>10<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwcA">]</span></span></a></sup> though it seems that the Spanish title was not legally recognized in France. Hugo later titled himself as a viscount, and it was as "Vicomte Victor Hugo" that he was appointed a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peerage_of_France" title="Peerage of France" id="mwcQ">peer of France</a> on 13 April 1845.<sup about="#mwt71" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-11" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-11"}}'><a href="#cite_note-11" id="mwcg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwcw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwdA">[</span>11<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwdQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt74" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Pairs_de_France_12-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Pairs de France"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Pairs_de_France-12"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Pairs_de_France-12" id="mwdg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwdw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mweA">[</span>12<span class="cite-bracket" id="mweQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mweg">Weary of the constant moving required by military life, Sophie separated temporarily from Léopold and settled in Paris in 1803 with her sons. There, she began seeing General <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Fanneau_de_La_Horie" title="Victor Fanneau de La Horie" id="mwew">Victor Fanneau de La Horie</a>, Hugo's godfather, who had been a comrade of General Hugo's during the campaign in Vendee. In October 1807, the family rejoined Leopold, now Colonel Hugo, Governor of the province of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Province_of_Avellino" title="Province of Avellino" id="mwfA">Avellino</a>. There, Hugo was taught mathematics by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_de_Samuele_Cagnazzi" title="Giuseppe de Samuele Cagnazzi" id="mwfQ">Giuseppe de Samuele Cagnazzi</a>, elder brother of Italian scientist <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luca_de_Samuele_Cagnazzi" title="Luca de Samuele Cagnazzi" id="mwfg">Luca de Samuele Cagnazzi</a>.<sup about="#mwt75" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-13" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-13"}}'><a href="#cite_note-13" id="mwfw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwgA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwgQ">[</span>13<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwgg">]</span></span></a></sup> Sophie found out that Leopold had been living in secret with an Englishwoman called Catherine Thomas.<sup about="#mwt78" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Stephens_2019_p._24_14-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Stephens 2019 p. 24"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Stephens_2019_p._24-14"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Stephens_2019_p._24-14" id="mwgw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwhA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwhQ">[</span>14<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwhg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt79" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwhw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Gallery\n","href":"./Template:Gallery"},"params":{"align":{"wt":"center"},"1":{"wt":"File:Sophie Trébuchet.jpg"},"2":{"wt":"[[Sophie Trébuchet]], mother of Victor Hugo\n"},"3":{"wt":"File:Général Joseph-Leopold Sigisbert Hugo.jpg"},"4":{"wt":"General [[Joseph Léopold Sigisbert Hugo|Joseph-Leopold Hugo]], father of Victor Hugo\n"}},"i":0}}]}'><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1275594942" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" about="#mwt80" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Gallery/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'>@media all and (max-width:720px){.mw-parser-output *{overflow:visible}.mw-parser-output .mod-gallery .gallerybox img{background:none!important}.mw-parser-output .mod-gallery .bordered-images .thumb img{outline:solid var(--background-color-neutral,#eaecf0)1px}.mw-parser-output .mod-gallery .whitebg .thumb{background:var(--background-color-base,#fff)!important}@media screen{html.skin-theme-clientpref-night .mw-parser-output .mod-gallery .bordered-images .thumb img[alt*="\200b \200b \200b "],html.skin-theme-clientpref-night .mw-parser-output .skin-invert-image .mod-gallery .whitebg .thumb.thumb.thumb img{outline:solid #d7d7d7 1px}html.skin-theme-clientpref-night .mw-parser-output .skin-invert-image .mod-gallery .whitebg .thumb.thumb.thumb .skin-invert-image .mod-gallery .whitebg .thumb.thumb.thumb img{outline:solid #d7d7d7 1px}html.skin-theme-clientpref-os .mw-parser-output .skin-invert-image .mod-gallery .whitebg .thumb.thumb.thumb img{background:none!important}html.skin-theme-clientpref-os .mw-parser-output .mod-gallery .whitebg .thumb img:not([alt*="\200b \200b \200b "]):not([alt*="\200b \200b \200c "]){background:white!important}html.skin-theme-clientpref-os .mw-parser-output .mod-gallery img[alt*="\200b \200b \200b "]{filter:invert(1)hue-rotate(180deg)}}</style></span><div class="mod-gallery mod-gallery-default mod-gallery-center" about="#mwt79" id="mwiA"><div class="main"><div><ul class="gallery mw-gallery-traditional nochecker bordered-images whitebg" typeof="mw:Extension/gallery" about="#mwt81" id="mwiQ" data-mw='{"name":"gallery","attrs":{"class":"nochecker bordered-images whitebg","heights":"180","widths":"180"},"body":{}}'>
<li class="gallerybox" style="width: 215px;" id="mwig"><div class="thumb" style="width: 210px; height: 210px;" id="mwiw"><span typeof="mw:File" id="mwjA"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Sophie_Trébuchet.jpg" class="mw-file-description" title="Sophie Trébuchet, mother of Victor Hugo" id="mwjQ"><img alt="Sophie Trébuchet, mother of Victor Hugo" resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Sophie_Trébuchet.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Sophie_Tr%C3%A9buchet.jpg/250px-Sophie_Tr%C3%A9buchet.jpg" decoding="async" data-file-width="3110" data-file-height="3595" data-file-type="bitmap" height="180" width="156" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Sophie_Tr%C3%A9buchet.jpg/330px-Sophie_Tr%C3%A9buchet.jpg 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwjg"/></a></span></div><div class="gallerytext" id="mwjw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sophie_Trébuchet" title="Sophie Trébuchet" id="mwkA">Sophie Trébuchet</a>, mother of Victor Hugo</div></li>
<li class="gallerybox" style="width: 215px;" id="mwkQ"><div class="thumb" style="width: 210px; height: 210px;" id="mwkg"><span typeof="mw:File" id="mwkw"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Général_Joseph-Leopold_Sigisbert_Hugo.jpg" class="mw-file-description" title="General Joseph-Leopold Hugo, father of Victor Hugo" id="mwlA"><img alt="General Joseph-Leopold Hugo, father of Victor Hugo" resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Général_Joseph-Leopold_Sigisbert_Hugo.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/G%C3%A9n%C3%A9ral_Joseph-Leopold_Sigisbert_Hugo.jpg/250px-G%C3%A9n%C3%A9ral_Joseph-Leopold_Sigisbert_Hugo.jpg" decoding="async" data-file-width="2863" data-file-height="3444" data-file-type="bitmap" height="180" width="150" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/G%C3%A9n%C3%A9ral_Joseph-Leopold_Sigisbert_Hugo.jpg/330px-G%C3%A9n%C3%A9ral_Joseph-Leopold_Sigisbert_Hugo.jpg 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwlQ"/></a></span></div><div class="gallerytext" id="mwlg">General <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Léopold_Sigisbert_Hugo" title="Joseph Léopold Sigisbert Hugo" id="mwlw">Joseph-Leopold Hugo</a>, father of Victor Hugo</div></li>
</ul></div></div></div>
<p id="mwmA">Soon, Hugo's father was called to Spain to fight the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peninsular_War" title="Peninsular War" id="mwmQ">Peninsular War</a>. Madame Hugo and her children were sent back to Paris in 1808, where they moved to an old convent, 12 <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt82" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwmg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Impasse des Feuillantines"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;">Impasse des Feuillantines</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt82" id="mwmw"/>, an isolated mansion in a deserted quarter of the left bank of the Seine. Hiding in a chapel at the back of the garden was de La Horie, who had conspired to restore the Bourbons and been condemned to death a few years earlier. He became a mentor to Hugo and his brothers.<sup about="#mwt85" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Caussé_p._4_15-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Caussé p. 4"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Caussé_p._4-15"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Caussé_p._4-15" id="mwnA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwnQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwng">[</span>15<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwnw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwoA">In 1811, the family joined their father in Spain. Hugo and his brothers were sent to school in Madrid at the <span title="Spanish-language text" about="#mwt86" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwoQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"es"},"2":{"wt":"Real Colegio de San Antonio de Abad"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="es">Real Colegio de San Antonio de Abad</i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_Spanish-language_text" about="#mwt86" id="mwog"/> while Sophie returned to Paris on her own, now officially separated from her husband. In 1812, as the Peninsular War was turning against France, de La Horie was arrested and executed. In February 1815, Hugo and Eugène were taken away from their mother and placed by their father in the Pension Cordier, a private boarding school in Paris, where Hugo and Eugène remained for three years while also attending lectures at <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lycée_Louis-le-Grand" title="Lycée Louis-le-Grand" id="mwow">Lycée Louis le Grand.</a><sup about="#mwt89" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Robb_2017_p._49_16-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Robb 2017 p. 49"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Robb_2017_p._49-16"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Robb_2017_p._49-16" id="mwpA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwpQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwpg">[</span>16<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwpw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwqA"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Portrait_de_Victor_Hugo_sur_fond_de_Notre-Dame_de_Reims_-_Jean_Alaux.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwqQ"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Portrait_de_Victor_Hugo_sur_fond_de_Notre-Dame_de_Reims_-_Jean_Alaux.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Portrait_de_Victor_Hugo_sur_fond_de_Notre-Dame_de_Reims_-_Jean_Alaux.jpg/250px-Portrait_de_Victor_Hugo_sur_fond_de_Notre-Dame_de_Reims_-_Jean_Alaux.jpg" decoding="async" data-file-width="3894" data-file-height="4593" data-file-type="bitmap" height="295" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Portrait_de_Victor_Hugo_sur_fond_de_Notre-Dame_de_Reims_-_Jean_Alaux.jpg/500px-Portrait_de_Victor_Hugo_sur_fond_de_Notre-Dame_de_Reims_-_Jean_Alaux.jpg 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwqg"/></a><figcaption id="mwqw">Hugo by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Alaux" title="Jean Alaux" id="mwrA">Jean Alaux</a>, 1825</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwrQ">On 10 July 1816, Hugo wrote in his diary: "I shall be <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chateaubriand" title="Chateaubriand" class="mw-redirect" id="mwrg">Chateaubriand</a> or nothing." In 1817, he wrote a poem for a competition organised by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Académie_Française" title="Académie Française" id="mwrw">Academie Française</a>, for which he received an honorable mention. The Academicians refused to believe that he was only fifteen.<sup about="#mwt94" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-King_1878_p._11_17-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"King 1878 p. 11"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-King_1878_p._11-17"}}'><a href="#cite_note-King_1878_p._11-17" id="mwsA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwsQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwsg">[</span>17<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwsw">]</span></span></a></sup> Hugo moved in with his mother to 18 <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt90" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwtA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"rue des Petits-Augustins"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;">rue des Petits-Augustins</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt90" id="mwtQ"/> the following year and began attending law school. Hugo fell in love and secretly became engaged, against his mother's wishes, to his childhood friend <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adèle_Foucher" title="Adèle Foucher" id="mwtg">Adèle Foucher</a>. In June 1821, Sophie Trebuchet died, and Léopold married his long-time mistress Catherine Thomas a month later. Hugo married Adèle the following year. In 1819, Hugo and his brothers began publishing a periodical called <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt91" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwtw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Le Conservateur littéraire"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr">Le Conservateur littéraire</i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt91" id="mwuA"/>.<sup about="#mwt97" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Frey_Laster_Hugo_1999_p._201_18-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Frey Laster Hugo 1999 p. 201"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Frey_Laster_Hugo_1999_p._201-18"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Frey_Laster_Hugo_1999_p._201-18" id="mwuQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwug"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwuw">[</span>18<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwvA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="2" id="mwvQ" aria-labelledby="Career"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Career">Career</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Victor_Hugo&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Career"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwvg">Hugo published his first novel (<i id="mwvw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans_of_Iceland" title="Hans of Iceland" id="mwwA">Hans of Iceland</a></i>, 1823) the year following his marriage, and his second three years later (<i id="mwwQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bug-Jargal" title="Bug-Jargal" id="mwwg">Bug-Jargal</a></i>, 1826). Between 1829 and 1840, he published five more volumes of poetry: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_Orientales" title="Les Orientales" id="mwww">Les Orientales</a>, 1829; <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_Feuilles_d'automne" title="Les Feuilles d'automne" id="mwxA">Les Feuilles d'automne</a>, 1831; <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_Chants_du_crépuscule?action=edit&amp;redlink=1" title="Les Chants du crépuscule (page does not exist)" about="#mwt98" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:LocalizedAttrs" class="new" id="mwxQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"ill","href":"./Template:Ill"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Les Chants du crépuscule"},"2":{"wt":"fr"}},"i":0}}]}' data-mw-i18n='{"title":{"lang":"x-page","key":"red-link-title","params":["Les Chants du crépuscule"]}}'>Les Chants du crépuscule</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal; " about="#mwt98" id="mwxg"><span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><a rel="mw:WikiLink/Interwiki" href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les%20Chants%20du%20crépuscule" title="fr:Les Chants du crépuscule" class="extiw">fr</a><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></span>, 1835; <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_Voix_intérieures" title="Les Voix intérieures" id="mwxw">Les Voix intérieures</a>, 1837; and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_Rayons_et_les_Ombres" title="Les Rayons et les Ombres" id="mwyA">Les Rayons et les Ombres</a>, 1840. This cemented his reputation as one of the greatest elegiac and lyric poets of his time.</p>

<p id="mwyQ">Like many young writers of his generation, Hugo was profoundly influenced by <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt99" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwyg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[François-René de Chateaubriand]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/François-René_de_Chateaubriand" title="François-René de Chateaubriand">François-René de Chateaubriand</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt99" id="mwyw"/>, the famous figure in the literary movement of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanticism" title="Romanticism" id="mwzA">Romanticism</a> and France's pre-eminent literary figure during the early 19th century. In his youth, Hugo resolved to be "<span title="French-language text" about="#mwt100" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwzQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Chateaubriand"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;">Chateaubriand</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt100" id="mwzg"/> or nothing", and his life would come to parallel that of his predecessor in many ways. Like <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt101" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwzw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Chateaubriand"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;">Chateaubriand</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt101" id="mw0A"/>, Hugo furthered the cause of Romanticism, became involved in politics (though mostly as a champion of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republicanism" title="Republicanism" id="mw0Q">Republicanism</a>), and was forced into exile due to his political stances. Along with Chateaubriand he attended the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coronation_of_Charles_X" title="Coronation of Charles X" class="mw-redirect" id="mw0g">Coronation of Charles X</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reims" title="Reims" id="mw0w">Reims</a> in 1825.<sup about="#mwt102" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-19" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-19"}}'><a href="#cite_note-19" id="mw1A"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw1Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw1g">[</span>19<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw1w">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mw2A">The precocious passion and eloquence of Hugo's early work brought success and fame at an early age. His first collection of poetry (<span title="French-language text" about="#mwt103" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw2Q" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Odes et poésies diverses]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odes_et_poésies_diverses" title="Odes et poésies diverses" class="mw-redirect">Odes et poésies diverses</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt103" id="mw2g"/>) was published in 1822 when he was only 20 years old and earned him a royal pension from <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_XVIII" title="Louis XVIII" id="mw2w">Louis XVIII</a>. Though the poems were admired for their spontaneous fervor and fluency, the collection that followed four years later in 1826 (<span title="French-language text" about="#mwt104" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw3A" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Odes et Ballades]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odes_et_Ballades" title="Odes et Ballades">Odes et Ballades</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt104" id="mw3Q"/>) revealed Hugo to be a great poet, a natural master of lyric and creative song.</p>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mw3g"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Achille_Devéria,_Victor_Hugo,_1829,_NGA_208390.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mw3w"><img alt="Image of Victor Hugo" resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Achille_Devéria,_Victor_Hugo,_1829,_NGA_208390.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Achille_Dev%C3%A9ria%2C_Victor_Hugo%2C_1829%2C_NGA_208390.jpg/250px-Achille_Dev%C3%A9ria%2C_Victor_Hugo%2C_1829%2C_NGA_208390.jpg" decoding="async" data-file-width="3231" data-file-height="4000" data-file-type="bitmap" height="248" width="200" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a9/Achille_Dev%C3%A9ria%2C_Victor_Hugo%2C_1829%2C_NGA_208390.jpg/500px-Achille_Dev%C3%A9ria%2C_Victor_Hugo%2C_1829%2C_NGA_208390.jpg 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mw4A"/></a><figcaption id="mw4Q">Victor Hugo in 1829, lithograph by Achille Devéria in the collection of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Gallery_of_Art" title="National Gallery of Art" id="mw4g">National Gallery of Art</a>, Washington D.C.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mw4w"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Monsieur_Madeleine_par_Gustave_Brion.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mw5A"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Monsieur_Madeleine_par_Gustave_Brion.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/Monsieur_Madeleine_par_Gustave_Brion.jpg/250px-Monsieur_Madeleine_par_Gustave_Brion.jpg" decoding="async" data-file-width="3195" data-file-height="4725" data-file-type="bitmap" height="370" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/Monsieur_Madeleine_par_Gustave_Brion.jpg/500px-Monsieur_Madeleine_par_Gustave_Brion.jpg 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mw5Q"/></a><figcaption id="mw5g"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Valjean" title="Jean Valjean" id="mw5w">Jean Valjean</a> (also known as Monsieur Madeleine in the book)<sup about="#mwt107" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-20" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-20"}}'><a href="#cite_note-20" id="mw6A"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw6Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw6g">[</span>20<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw6w">]</span></span></a></sup> is the principal character in Hugo's great novel, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_Misérables" title="Les Misérables" id="mw7A">Les Misérables</a>.</figcaption></figure>

<p id="mw7Q">Victor Hugo's first mature work of fiction was published in February 1829 by Charles Gosselin without the author's name and reflected the acute social conscience that would infuse his later work. <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt108" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw7g" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Le Dernier jour d&apos;un condamné"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr">Le Dernier jour d'un condamné</i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt108" id="mw7w"/> (<i id="mw8A"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Last_Day_of_a_Condemned_Man" title="The Last Day of a Condemned Man" id="mw8Q">The Last Day of a Condemned Man</a></i>) would have a profound influence on later writers such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albert_Camus" title="Albert Camus" id="mw8g">Albert Camus</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Dickens" title="Charles Dickens" id="mw8w">Charles Dickens</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fyodor_Dostoyevsky" title="Fyodor Dostoyevsky" class="mw-redirect" id="mw9A">Fyodor Dostoyevsky</a>. <i id="mw9Q">Claude Gueux,</i> a documentary short story about a real-life murderer who had been executed in France, was published in 1834. Hugo himself later considered it to be a precursor to his great work on social injustice, <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt109" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw9g" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Les Misérables]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_Misérables" title="Les Misérables">Les Misérables</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt109" id="mw9w"/>.<sup about="#mwt113" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-21" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-21"}}'><a href="#cite_note-21" id="mw-A"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw-Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw-g">[</span>21<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw-w">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mw_A">Hugo became the figurehead of the Romantic literary movement with the plays <i id="mw_Q">Cromwell</i> (1827) and <i id="mw_g">Hernani</i> (1830).<sup about="#mwt117" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-22" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-22"}}'><a href="#cite_note-22" id="mw_w"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAQA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQE">[</span>22<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQI">]</span></span></a></sup> <i id="mwAQM">Hernani</i> announced the arrival of French romanticism: performed at the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comédie-Française" title="Comédie-Française" id="mwAQQ">Comédie-Française</a>, it was greeted with several nights of rioting as romantics and traditionalists clashed over the play's deliberate disregard for neo-classical rules. Hugo's popularity as a playwright grew with subsequent plays, such as <i id="mwAQU">Marion Delorme</i> (1831), <i id="mwAQY"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_roi_s'amuse" title="Le roi s'amuse" id="mwAQc">Le roi s'amuse</a></i> (1832), and <i id="mwAQg">Ruy Blas</i> (1838).<sup about="#mwt118" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-23" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-23"}}'><a href="#cite_note-23" id="mwAQk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAQo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQs">[</span>23<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQw">]</span></span></a></sup> Hugo's novel <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt114" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAQ0" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Notre-Dame de Paris"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr">Notre-Dame de Paris</i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt114" id="mwAQ4"/> (<i id="mwAQ8"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hunchback_of_Notre-Dame" title="The Hunchback of Notre-Dame" id="mwARA">The Hunchback of Notre-Dame</a></i>) was published in 1831 and quickly translated into other languages across Europe. One of the effects of the novel was to shame the City of Paris into restoring the much neglected <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Notre_Dame_de_Paris" title="Notre Dame de Paris" class="mw-redirect" id="mwARE">Cathedral of Notre Dame</a>, which was attracting thousands of tourists who had read the popular novel.</p>

<p id="mwARI">Hugo began planning a major novel about social misery and injustice as early as the 1830s, but a full 17 years were needed for <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt119" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwARM" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Les Misérables"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr">Les Misérables</i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt119" id="mwARQ"/> to be realised and finally published in 1862. Hugo had previously used the departure of prisoners for the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bagne_of_Toulon" title="Bagne of Toulon" id="mwARU">Bagne of Toulon</a> in <i id="mwARY">Le Dernier Jour d'un condamné</i>. He went to Toulon to visit the Bagne in 1839 and took extensive notes, though he did not start writing the book until 1845. On one of the pages of his notes about the prison, he wrote in large block letters a possible name for his hero: "JEAN TRÉJEAN". When the book was finally written, Tréjean became <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Valjean" title="Jean Valjean" id="mwARc">Jean Valjean</a>.<sup about="#mwt122" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-24" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-24"}}'><a href="#cite_note-24" id="mwARg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwARk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARo">[</span>24<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARs">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwARw">Hugo was acutely aware of the quality of the novel, as evidenced in a letter he wrote to his publisher, Albert Lacroix, on 23 March 1862: "My conviction is that this book is going to be one of the peaks, if not the crowning point of my work."<sup about="#mwt127" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-25" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-25"}}'><a href="#cite_note-25" id="mwAR0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAR4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAR8">[</span>25<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASA">]</span></span></a></sup> Publication of <i id="mwASE">Les Misérables</i> went to the highest bidder. The Belgian publishing house <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt123" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwASI" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Lacroix"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;">Lacroix</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt123" id="mwASM"/> and <span title="Dutch-language text" about="#mwt124" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwASQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"nl"},"2":{"wt":"Verboeckhoven"},"nocat":{"wt":"y"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="nl">Verboeckhoven</i></span> undertook a marketing campaign unusual for the time, issuing press releases about the work a full six months before the launch. It also initially published only the first part of the novel ("<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fantine" title="Fantine" id="mwASU">Fantine</a>"), which was launched simultaneously in major cities. Installments of the book sold out within hours and had an enormous impact on French society.</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwASY"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Ebcosette.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwASc"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Ebcosette.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/Ebcosette.jpg/250px-Ebcosette.jpg" decoding="async" data-file-width="709" data-file-height="1122" data-file-type="bitmap" height="396" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/Ebcosette.jpg/500px-Ebcosette.jpg 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwASg"/></a><figcaption id="mwASk">Illustration by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Émile_Bayard" title="Émile Bayard" id="mwASo">Émile Bayard</a> from the original edition of <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt128" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwASs" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Les Misérables]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_Misérables" title="Les Misérables">Les Misérables</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt128" id="mwASw"/> (1862)</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwAS0"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Victor_Hugo-Hunchback.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwAS4"><img alt="" resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Victor_Hugo-Hunchback.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6f/Victor_Hugo-Hunchback.jpg/250px-Victor_Hugo-Hunchback.jpg" decoding="async" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="1170" data-file-type="bitmap" height="293" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6f/Victor_Hugo-Hunchback.jpg/500px-Victor_Hugo-Hunchback.jpg 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwAS8"/></a><figcaption id="mwATA">Illustration by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luc-Olivier_Merson" title="Luc-Olivier Merson" id="mwATE">Luc-Olivier Merson</a> for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hunchback_of_Notre-Dame" title="The Hunchback of Notre-Dame" id="mwATI">Notre-Dame de Paris</a> (1881)</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwATM">The critical establishment was generally hostile to the novel; <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt129" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwATQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Hippolyte Taine|Taine]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hippolyte_Taine" title="Hippolyte Taine">Taine</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt129" id="mwATU"/> found it insincere, <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt130" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwATY" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Jules Barbey d&apos;Aurevilly|Barbey d&apos;Aurevilly]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jules_Barbey_d'Aurevilly" title="Jules Barbey d'Aurevilly">Barbey d'Aurevilly</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt130" id="mwATc"/> complained of its vulgarity, <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt131" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwATg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Gustave Flaubert]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gustave_Flaubert" title="Gustave Flaubert">Gustave Flaubert</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt131" id="mwATk"/> found within it "neither truth nor greatness", the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Goncourt_brothers" title="Goncourt brothers" id="mwATo"><span title="French-language text" about="#mwt133" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwATs" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Goncourt"},"nocat":{"wt":"y"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr">Goncourt</i></span> brothers</a> lambasted its artificiality, and <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt132" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwATw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Charles Baudelaire|Baudelaire]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Baudelaire" title="Charles Baudelaire">Baudelaire</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt132" id="mwAT0"/>—despite giving favourable reviews in newspapers—castigated it in private as "repulsive and inept". However, <i id="mwAT4">Les Misérables</i> proved popular enough with the masses that the issues it highlighted were soon on the agenda of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_of_France" title="National Assembly of France" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAT8">National Assembly of France</a>. Today, the novel remains his best-known work. It is popular worldwide and has been adapted for cinema, television, and stage shows.</p>

<p id="mwAUA">An apocryphal tale<sup about="#mwt141" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-26" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-26"}}'><a href="#cite_note-26" id="mwAUE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAUI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUM">[</span>26<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUQ">]</span></span></a></sup> has circulated, describing the shortest correspondence in history as having been between Hugo and his publisher <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hurst_and_Blackett" title="Hurst and Blackett" id="mwAUU">Hurst and Blackett</a> in 1862. Hugo was on vacation when <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt134" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAUY" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Les Misérables"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr">Les Misérables</i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt134" id="mwAUc"/> was published. He queried the reaction to the work by sending a single-character <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telegram" title="Telegram" id="mwAUg">telegram</a> to his publisher, asking <style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r886049734" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt135" id="mwAUk" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Mono/styles.css"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mono","href":"./Template:Mono"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"?"}},"i":0}}]}'>.mw-parser-output .monospaced{font-family:monospace,monospace}</style><span class="monospaced" about="#mwt135" id="mwAUo">?</span>. The publisher replied with a single <link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r886049734" about="#mwt137" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" id="mwAUs" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Mono/styles.css"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mono","href":"./Template:Mono"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"!"}},"i":0}}]}'/><span class="monospaced" about="#mwt137" id="mwAUw">!</span> to indicate its success.<sup about="#mwt142" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-27" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-27"}}'><a href="#cite_note-27" id="mwAU0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAU4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAU8">[</span>27<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVA">]</span></span></a></sup> Hugo turned away from social/political issues in his next novel, <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt139" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAVE" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Les Travailleurs de la Mer"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr">Les Travailleurs de la Mer</i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt139" id="mwAVI"/> (<i id="mwAVM"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toilers_of_the_Sea" title="Toilers of the Sea" id="mwAVQ">Toilers of the Sea</a></i>), published in 1866. The book was well received, perhaps due to the previous success of <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt140" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAVU" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Les Misérables"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr">Les Misérables</i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt140" id="mwAVY"/>. Dedicated to the channel island of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guernsey" title="Guernsey" id="mwAVc">Guernsey</a>, where Hugo spent 15 years of exile, the novel tells of a man who attempts to win the approval of his beloved's father by rescuing his ship, intentionally marooned by its captain who hopes to escape with a treasure of money it is transporting, through an exhausting battle of human engineering against the force of the sea and an almost mythical beast of the sea, a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giant_squid_in_popular_culture" title="Giant squid in popular culture" id="mwAVg">giant squid</a>. Superficially an adventure, one of Hugo's biographers calls it a "metaphor for the 19th century–technical progress, creative genius and hard work overcoming the immanent evil of the material world."<sup about="#mwt145" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-28" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-28"}}'><a href="#cite_note-28" id="mwAVk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAVo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVs">[</span>28<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAV0">The word used in Guernsey to refer to squid (<span title="French-language text" about="#mwt146" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAV4" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"pieuvre"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr">pieuvre</i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt146" id="mwAV8"/>, also sometimes applied to octopus) was to enter the French language as a result of its use in the book. Hugo returned to political and social issues in his next novel, <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt147" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAWA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"L&apos;Homme Qui Rit"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr">L'Homme Qui Rit</i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt147" id="mwAWE"/> (<i id="mwAWI"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Man_Who_Laughs" title="The Man Who Laughs" id="mwAWM">The Man Who Laughs</a></i>), which was published in 1869 and painted a critical picture of the aristocracy. The novel was not as successful as his previous efforts, and Hugo himself began to comment on the growing distance between himself and literary contemporaries such as <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt148" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAWQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Gustave Flaubert|Flaubert]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gustave_Flaubert" title="Gustave Flaubert">Flaubert</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt148" id="mwAWU"/> and <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt149" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAWY" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Émile Zola]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Émile_Zola" title="Émile Zola">Émile Zola</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt149" id="mwAWc"/>, whose <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literary_realism" title="Literary realism" id="mwAWg">realist</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Naturalism_(literature)" title="Naturalism (literature)" id="mwAWk">naturalist</a> novels were now exceeding the popularity of his own work.</p>

<p id="mwAWo">His last novel, <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt150" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAWs" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Quatre-vingt-treize"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr">Quatre-vingt-treize</i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt150" id="mwAWw"/> (<i id="mwAW0"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ninety-Three" title="Ninety-Three" id="mwAW4">Ninety-Three</a></i>), published in 1874, dealt with a subject that Hugo had previously avoided: the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reign_of_Terror" title="Reign of Terror" id="mwAW8">Reign of Terror</a> during the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Revolution" title="French Revolution" id="mwAXA">French Revolution</a>. Though Hugo's popularity was on the decline at the time of its publication, many now consider <i id="mwAXE">Ninety-Three</i> to be a work on par with Hugo's better-known novels.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="3" id="mwAXI" aria-labelledby="Political_life_and_self-exile"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Political_life_and_self-exile">Political life and self-exile</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Victor_Hugo&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Political life and self-exile"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwAXM"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Maison_natale_Victor_Hugo_079.JPG" class="mw-file-description" id="mwAXQ"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Maison_natale_Victor_Hugo_079.JPG" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Maison_natale_Victor_Hugo_079.JPG/250px-Maison_natale_Victor_Hugo_079.JPG" decoding="async" data-file-width="1322" data-file-height="1492" data-file-type="bitmap" height="282" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Maison_natale_Victor_Hugo_079.JPG/500px-Maison_natale_Victor_Hugo_079.JPG 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwAXU"/></a><figcaption id="mwAXY">Portrait as member of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_(France)" title="National Assembly (France)" id="mwAXc">National Assembly</a> of the Second Republic, 1848</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwAXg">After three unsuccessful attempts, Hugo was finally elected to the <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt151" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAXk" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Académie française]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Académie_française" title="Académie française" class="mw-redirect">Académie française</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt151" id="mwAXo"/> in 1841, solidifying his position in the world of French arts and letters. A group of French academicians, particularly <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt152" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAXs" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Victor-Joseph Étienne de Jouy|Étienne de Jouy]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor-Joseph_Étienne_de_Jouy" title="Victor-Joseph Étienne de Jouy">Étienne de Jouy</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt152" id="mwAXw"/>, were fighting against the "romantic evolution" and had managed to delay Victor Hugo's election.<sup about="#mwt157" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-29" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-29"}}'><a href="#cite_note-29" id="mwAX0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAX4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAX8">[</span>29<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAYA">]</span></span></a></sup> Thereafter, he became increasingly involved in French politics.</p>

<p id="mwAYE">On the nomination of King <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt158" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAYI" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Louis Philippe I]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Philippe_I" title="Louis Philippe I">Louis Philippe I</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt158" id="mwAYM"/>, Hugo entered the Upper Chamber of Parliament as a <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt159" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAYQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[pair de France]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pair_de_France" title="Pair de France" class="mw-redirect">pair de France</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt159" id="mwAYU"/> in 1845, where he spoke against the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_punishment_in_France" title="Capital punishment in France" id="mwAYY">death penalty</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_injustice" title="Social injustice" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAYc">social injustice</a>, and in favour of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_the_press" title="Freedom of the press" id="mwAYg">freedom of the press</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-government" title="Self-government" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAYk">self-government</a> for Poland.</p>

<p id="mwAYo">In 1848, Hugo was <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1848_French_Constituent_Assembly_election" title="1848 French Constituent Assembly election" id="mwAYs">elected to the National Assembly</a> of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Second_Republic" title="French Second Republic" id="mwAYw">Second Republic</a> as a conservative. In 1849, he broke with the conservatives when he gave a noted speech calling for the end of misery and poverty. Other speeches called for universal suffrage and free education for all children. Hugo's advocacy to abolish the death penalty was renowned internationally.<sup about="#mwt160" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-31" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"note"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-31"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"refn","href":"./Template:Refn"},"params":{"group":{"wt":"note"},"1":{"wt":"These parliamentary speeches are published in {{lang|fr|Œuvres complètes: actes et paroles I : avant l&apos;exil, 1841–1851}}. Scroll down to the {{lang|fr|Assemblée Constituante 1848}} heading and subsequent pages.&lt;ref>{{cite web |publisher=[[State Library of Victoria]] |title=Victor Hugo: Les Misérables – From Page to Stage research guide |url=http://guides.slv.vic.gov.au/content.php?pid=572687&amp;sid=4722472 |access-date=3 July 2014 |archive-date=3 April 2015 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150403010902/http://guides.slv.vic.gov.au/content.php?pid=572687&amp;sid=4722472 |url-status=dead }}&lt;/ref>"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-31" data-mw-group="note" id="mwAY0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAY4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAY8">[</span>note 1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAZA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwAZE"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Charles_Hugo_-_Victor_Hugo_on_the_Rock_of_the_Exiles_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwAZI"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Charles_Hugo_-_Victor_Hugo_on_the_Rock_of_the_Exiles_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e1/Charles_Hugo_-_Victor_Hugo_on_the_Rock_of_the_Exiles_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg/250px-Charles_Hugo_-_Victor_Hugo_on_the_Rock_of_the_Exiles_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg" decoding="async" data-file-width="1823" data-file-height="3073" data-file-type="bitmap" height="421" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e1/Charles_Hugo_-_Victor_Hugo_on_the_Rock_of_the_Exiles_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg/500px-Charles_Hugo_-_Victor_Hugo_on_the_Rock_of_the_Exiles_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwAZM"/></a><figcaption id="mwAZQ">Among the Rocks in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey" title="Jersey" id="mwAZU">Jersey</a> (1853–1855)</figcaption></figure>

<p id="mwAZY">When Louis Napoleon (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleon_III" title="Napoleon III" id="mwAZc">Napoleon III</a>) <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_coup_d'état_of_1851" title="French coup d'état of 1851" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAZg">seized complete power in 1851</a>, establishing an anti-parliamentary constitution, Hugo openly declared him a traitor to France. He moved to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels" title="Brussels" id="mwAZk">Brussels</a>, then <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jersey" title="Jersey" id="mwAZo">Jersey</a>, from which he was expelled for supporting <i id="mwAZs">L'Homme</i>, a local newspaper that had published a letter to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_Victoria" title="Queen Victoria" id="mwAZw">Queen Victoria</a> by a French republican deemed <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treason" title="Treason" id="mwAZ0">treasonous</a>. He finally settled with his family at <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hauteville_House" title="Hauteville House" id="mwAZ4">Hauteville House</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saint_Peter_Port" title="Saint Peter Port" id="mwAZ8">Saint Peter Port</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guernsey" title="Guernsey" id="mwAaA">Guernsey</a>, where he would live in self-imposed exile from October 1855 until 1870.</p>

<p id="mwAaE">While in self-exile, Hugo published his famous political pamphlets against Napoleon III, <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt170" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAaI" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Napoléon le Petit]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoléon_le_Petit" title="Napoléon le Petit">Napoléon le Petit</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt170" id="mwAaM"/> and <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt171" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAaQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Histoire d&apos;un crime]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Histoire_d'un_crime" title="Histoire d'un crime" class="mw-redirect">Histoire d'un crime</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt171" id="mwAaU"/>. The pamphlets were banned in France but nonetheless had a strong impact there. He also composed or published some of his best work during his period in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guernsey" title="Guernsey" id="mwAaY">Guernsey</a>, including <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt172" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAac" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Les Misérables]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_Misérables" title="Les Misérables">Les Misérables</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt172" id="mwAag"/> and three widely praised collections of poetry (<span title="French-language text" about="#mwt173" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAak" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Les Châtiments]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_Châtiments" title="Les Châtiments">Les Châtiments</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt173" id="mwAao"/>, 1853; <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt174" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAas" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Les Contemplations]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_Contemplations" title="Les Contemplations">Les Contemplations</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt174" id="mwAaw"/>, 1856; and <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt175" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAa0" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[La Légende des siècles]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Légende_des_siècles" title="La Légende des siècles">La Légende des siècles</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt175" id="mwAa4"/>, 1859).</p>

<p id="mwAa8">However, Víctor Hugo said in <i id="mwAbA">Les Misérables</i>:<sup about="#mwt178" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-32" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-32"}}'><a href="#cite_note-32" id="mwAbE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAbI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbM">[</span>31<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAbQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>
