<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1244412712" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt179" id="mwAbU" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Template:Blockquote/styles.css"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Blockquote","href":"./Template:Blockquote"},"params":{"text":{"wt":"The wretched moral character of Thénardier, the frustrated bourgeois, was hopeless; it was in America what it had been in Europe. Contact with a wicked man is sometimes enough to rot a good deed and cause something bad to come out of it. With Marius&apos;s money, Thénardier became a slave trader."}},"i":0}}]}'>.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 32px}.mw-parser-output .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;margin-top:0}@media(min-width:500px){.mw-parser-output .templatequotecite{padding-left:1.6em}}</style><blockquote class="templatequote " about="#mwt179" id="mwAbY"><p>The wretched moral character of Thénardier, the frustrated bourgeois, was hopeless; it was in America what it had been in Europe. Contact with a wicked man is sometimes enough to rot a good deed and cause something bad to come out of it. With Marius's money, Thénardier became a slave trader.</p></blockquote>

<p id="mwAbc">Like most of his contemporaries, Hugo justified colonialism in terms of a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civilizing" title="Civilizing" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAbg">civilizing</a> mission and putting an end to the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barbary_slave_trade" title="Barbary slave trade" id="mwAbk">slave trade</a> on the Barbary coast. In a speech delivered on 18 May 1879, during a banquet to celebrate the abolition of slavery, in the presence of the French abolitionist writer and parliamentarian Victor Schœlcher, Hugo declared that the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_Sea" title="Mediterranean Sea" id="mwAbo">Mediterranean Sea</a> formed a natural divide between "ultimate civilisation and... utter barbarism." Hugo declared that "God offers Africa to Europe. Take it" and "in the nineteenth century the white man made a man out of the black, in the twentieth century Europe will make a world out of Africa".<sup about="#mwt183" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-33" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-33"}}'><a href="#cite_note-33" id="mwAbs"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAbw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAb0">[</span>32<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAb4">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAb8">This might partly explain why, in spite of his deep interest and involvement in political matters, he remained silent on the Algerian issue. He knew about the atrocities committed by the French Army during the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_conquest_of_Algeria" title="French conquest of Algeria" id="mwAcA">French conquest of Algeria</a> as evidenced by his diary<sup about="#mwt186" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-34" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-34"}}'><a href="#cite_note-34" id="mwAcE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAcI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcM">[</span>33<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcQ">]</span></span></a></sup> but he never denounced them publicly; however, in <i id="mwAcU">Les Misérables</i>, Hugo wrote: "Algeria too harshly conquered, and, as in the case of India by the English, with more barbarism than civilization."<sup about="#mwt189" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-35" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-35"}}'><a href="#cite_note-35" id="mwAcY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAcc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAcg">[</span>34<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAck">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwAco"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Victor_Hugo_Bruxelles_Radoux_1861.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwAcs"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Victor_Hugo_Bruxelles_Radoux_1861.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/Victor_Hugo_Bruxelles_Radoux_1861.jpg/250px-Victor_Hugo_Bruxelles_Radoux_1861.jpg" decoding="async" data-file-width="3152" data-file-height="3880" data-file-type="bitmap" height="308" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/Victor_Hugo_Bruxelles_Radoux_1861.jpg/500px-Victor_Hugo_Bruxelles_Radoux_1861.jpg 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwAcw"/></a><figcaption id="mwAc0">Victor Hugo in 1861</figcaption></figure><p id="mwAc4">
After coming in contact with <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victor_Schœlcher" title="Victor Schœlcher" id="mwAc8">Victor Schœlcher</a>, a writer who fought for the abolition of slavery and French colonialism in the Caribbean, he started strongly campaigning against slavery. In a letter to American abolitionist <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Weston_Chapman" title="Maria Weston Chapman" id="mwAdA">Maria Weston Chapman</a>, on 6 July 1851, Hugo wrote: "Slavery in the United States! It is the duty of this republic to set such a bad example no longer... The United States must renounce slavery, or they must renounce liberty."<sup about="#mwt190" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHugoAmerican_Anti-Slavery_Society18607_36-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTEHugoAmerican Anti-Slavery Society18607"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEHugoAmerican_Anti-Slavery_Society18607-36"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Hugo"},"2":{"wt":"American Anti-Slavery Society"},"3":{"wt":"1860"},"p":{"wt":"7"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHugoAmerican_Anti-Slavery_Society18607-36" id="mwAdE"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAdI"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdM">[</span>35<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdQ">]</span></span></a></sup> In 1859, he wrote a letter asking the United States government, for the sake of their own reputation in the future, to spare abolitionist <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Brown_(abolitionist)" title="John Brown (abolitionist)" id="mwAdU">John Brown's</a> life, Hugo justified Brown's actions by these words: "Assuredly, if insurrection is ever a sacred duty, it must be when it is directed against Slavery."<sup about="#mwt193" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEl'Esclavage_37-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTEl&apos;Esclavage"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEl&apos;Esclavage-37"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"l&apos;Esclavage"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEl'Esclavage-37" id="mwAdY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAdc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdg">[</span>36<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdk">]</span></span></a></sup> Hugo agreed to diffuse and sell one of his best known drawings, "Le Pendu", an homage to John Brown, so one could "keep alive in souls the memory of this liberator of our black brothers, of this heroic martyr John Brown, who died for Christ just as Christ.<sup about="#mwt196" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHerrington2005131_38-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTEHerrington2005131"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTEHerrington2005131-38"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"sfn","href":"./Template:Sfn"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Herrington"},"2":{"wt":"2005"},"p":{"wt":"131"}},"i":0}}]}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHerrington2005131-38" id="mwAdo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAds"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAdw">[</span>37<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAd0">]</span></span></a></sup></p><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1244412712" about="#mwt199" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" id="mwAd4" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Template:Blockquote/styles.css"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Blockquote","href":"./Template:Blockquote"},"params":{"text":{"wt":"Only one slave on Earth is enough to dishonour the freedom of all men. So the abolition of slavery is, at this hour, the supreme goal of the thinkers."},"author":{"wt":"Victor Hugo"},"title":{"wt":"17 January 1862"},"source":{"wt":"{{sfn|Langellier|2014 |p=117}}"}},"i":0}}]}'/><blockquote class="templatequote " about="#mwt199"><p>Only one slave on Earth is enough to dishonour the freedom of all men. So the abolition of slavery is, at this hour, the supreme goal of the thinkers.</p></blockquote><div class="templatequotecite" about="#mwt199" id="mwAd8"><p style="display: inline; padding-left: 2.3em;">—<span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>Victor Hugo, 17 January 1862, <sup about="#mwt201" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELangellier2014117_39-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"group":"","name":"FOOTNOTELangellier2014117"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FOOTNOTELangellier2014117-39"}}'><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELangellier2014117-39" id="mwAeA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAeE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeI">[</span>38<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAeM">]</span></span></a></sup><span style="display:none" data-plural="1"></span></p></div>

<p id="mwAeQ">As a novelist, diarist, and member of Parliament, Victor Hugo fought a lifelong battle for the abolition of the death penalty. <i id="mwAeU"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Last_Day_of_a_Condemned_Man" title="The Last Day of a Condemned Man" id="mwAeY">The Last Day of a Condemned Man</a></i> published in 1829 analyses the pangs of a man awaiting execution; several entries in <i id="mwAec">Things Seen</i> (<i id="mwAeg">Choses vues</i>), the diary he kept between 1830 and 1885, convey his firm condemnation of what he regarded as a barbaric sentence;<sup about="#mwt205" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-40" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-40"}}'><a href="#cite_note-40" id="mwAek"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAeo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAes">[</span>39<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAew">]</span></span></a></sup> on 15 September 1848, seven months after the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Revolution_of_1848" title="French Revolution of 1848" id="mwAe0">Revolution of 1848</a>, he delivered a speech before the Assembly and concluded, "You have overthrown the throne. ... Now overthrow the scaffold."<sup about="#mwt208" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-41" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-41"}}'><a href="#cite_note-41" id="mwAe4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAe8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfA">[</span>40<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfE">]</span></span></a></sup> His influence was credited in the removal of the death penalty from the constitutions of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_punishment_in_Switzerland" title="Capital punishment in Switzerland" id="mwAfI">Geneva</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_punishment_in_Portugal" title="Capital punishment in Portugal" id="mwAfM">Portugal</a>, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombia" title="Colombia" id="mwAfQ">Colombia</a>.<sup about="#mwt211" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-42" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-42"}}'><a href="#cite_note-42" id="mwAfU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAfY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfc">[</span>41<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAfg">]</span></span></a></sup> He had also pleaded for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benito_Juárez" title="Benito Juárez" id="mwAfk">Benito Juárez</a> to spare the recently captured emperor <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maximilian_I_of_Mexico" title="Maximilian I of Mexico" id="mwAfo">Maximilian I of Mexico</a>, but to no avail.<sup about="#mwt214" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-43" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-43"}}'><a href="#cite_note-43" id="mwAfs"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAfw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAf0">[</span>42<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAf4">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAf8">Although Napoleon III granted an amnesty to all political exiles in 1859, Hugo declined, as it meant he would have to curtail his criticisms of the government. It was only after Napoleon III fell from power and the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Third_Republic" title="French Third Republic" id="mwAgA">Third Republic</a> was proclaimed that Hugo finally returned to his homeland in 1870, where he was promptly elected to the National Assembly and the Senate.</p>

<p id="mwAgE">He was in Paris during the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Paris_(1870–71)" title="Siege of Paris (1870–71)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAgI">siege by the Prussian Army in 1870</a>, famously eating animals given to him by the Paris Zoo. As the siege continued, and food became ever more scarce, he wrote in his diary that he was reduced to "eating the unknown".<sup about="#mwt217" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-44" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-44"}}'><a href="#cite_note-44" id="mwAgM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAgQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgU">[</span>43<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAgY">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwAgc"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Combats_dans_la_rue_Rivoli.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwAgg"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Combats_dans_la_rue_Rivoli.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Combats_dans_la_rue_Rivoli.jpg/250px-Combats_dans_la_rue_Rivoli.jpg" decoding="async" data-file-width="1198" data-file-height="917" data-file-type="bitmap" height="191" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/93/Combats_dans_la_rue_Rivoli.jpg/500px-Combats_dans_la_rue_Rivoli.jpg 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwAgk"/></a><figcaption id="mwAgo"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Commune" title="Paris Commune" id="mwAgs">Communards</a> defending a barricade on the Rue de Rivoli</figcaption></figure><p id="mwAgw">During the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paris_Commune" title="Paris Commune" id="mwAg0">Paris Commune</a>—the revolutionary government that took power on 18 March 1871 and was toppled on 28 May—Victor Hugo was harshly critical of the atrocities committed on both sides. On 9 April, he wrote in his diary, "In short, this Commune is as idiotic as the National Assembly is ferocious. From both sides, folly."<sup about="#mwt220" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-45" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-45"}}'><a href="#cite_note-45" id="mwAg4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAg8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhA">[</span>44<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhE">]</span></span></a></sup> Yet he made a point of offering his support to members of the Commune subjected to brutal repression. He had been in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brussels" title="Brussels" id="mwAhI">Brussels</a> since 22 March 1871 when in the 27 May issue of the Belgian newspaper <i id="mwAhM">l'Indépendance</i> Victor Hugo denounced the government's refusal to grant political asylum to the Communards threatened with imprisonment, banishment or execution.<sup about="#mwt223" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-46" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-46"}}'><a href="#cite_note-46" id="mwAhQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAhU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhY">[</span>45<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhc">]</span></span></a></sup> This caused so much uproar that in the evening a mob of fifty to sixty men attempted to force their way into the writer's house shouting, "Death to Victor Hugo! Hang him! Death to the scoundrel!"<sup about="#mwt226" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-47" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-47"}}'><a href="#cite_note-47" id="mwAhg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAhk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAho">[</span>46<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAhs">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAhw">Hugo, who said "A war between Europeans is a civil war,"<sup about="#mwt229" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-48" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-48"}}'><a href="#cite_note-48" id="mwAh0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAh4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAh8">[</span>47<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAiA">]</span></span></a></sup> was a strong advocate for the creation of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Federation" title="European Federation" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAiE">United States of Europe</a>. He expounded his views on the subject in a speech he delivered during the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Peace_Congress" title="International Peace Congress" id="mwAiI">International Peace Congress</a> which took place in Paris in 1849.<sup about="#mwt230" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-49" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-49"}}'><a href="#cite_note-49" id="mwAiM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAiQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAiU">[</span>48<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAiY">]</span></span></a></sup> Here he declared "nations of the continent, will, without losing your distinctive qualities and glorious individuality, be blended into a superior unity and constitute a European fraternity, just as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Normandy" title="Normandy" id="mwAic">Normandy</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brittany" title="Brittany" id="mwAig">Brittany</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burgundy" title="Burgundy" id="mwAik">Burgundy</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lorraine" title="Lorraine" id="mwAio">Lorraine</a>, have been blended into France", this new state would be administered by "a great Sovereign senate which will be to Europe what the Parliament is to England".<sup about="#mwt233" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-50" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-50"}}'><a href="#cite_note-50" id="mwAis"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAiw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAi0">[</span>49<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAi4">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAi8">Because of his concern for the rights of artists and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copyright" title="Copyright" id="mwAjA">copyright</a>, he was a founding member of the <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt234" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAjE" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Association Littéraire et Artistique Internationale]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_Littéraire_et_Artistique_Internationale" title="Association Littéraire et Artistique Internationale">Association Littéraire et Artistique Internationale</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt234" id="mwAjI"/>, which led to the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berne_Convention_for_the_Protection_of_Literary_and_Artistic_Works" title="Berne Convention for the Protection of Literary and Artistic Works" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAjM">Berne Convention for the Protection of Literary and Artistic Works</a>. However, in <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt235" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAjQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Pauvert"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;">Pauvert</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt235" id="mwAjU"/>'s published archives, he states strongly that "any work of art has two authors: the people who confusingly feel something, a creator who translates these feelings, and the people again who consecrate his vision of that feeling. When one of the authors dies, the rights should totally be granted back to the other, the people." He was one of the earlier supporters of the concept of <i id="mwAjY"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domaine_public_payant" title="Domaine public payant" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAjc">domaine public payant</a></i>, under which a nominal fee would be charged for copying or performing works in the public domain, and this would go into a common fund dedicated to helping artists, especially young people.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="4" id="mwAjg" aria-labelledby="Religious_views"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Religious_views">Religious views</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Victor_Hugo&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Religious views"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwAjk"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Glyptotek-Rodin-Victor_Hugo.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwAjo"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Glyptotek-Rodin-Victor_Hugo.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Glyptotek-Rodin-Victor_Hugo.jpg/250px-Glyptotek-Rodin-Victor_Hugo.jpg" decoding="async" data-file-width="3433" data-file-height="5150" data-file-type="bitmap" height="375" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/22/Glyptotek-Rodin-Victor_Hugo.jpg/500px-Glyptotek-Rodin-Victor_Hugo.jpg 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwAjs"/></a><figcaption id="mwAjw">Head of Victor Hugo modeled by the sculptor <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auguste_Rodin" title="Auguste Rodin" id="mwAj0">Auguste Rodin</a> and conserved at the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ny_Carlsberg_Glyptotek" title="Ny Carlsberg Glyptotek" id="mwAj4">Ny Carlsberg Glyptotek</a> in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copenhagen" title="Copenhagen" id="mwAj8">Copenhagen</a></figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwAkA">Hugo's religious views changed radically over the course of his life. In his youth and under the influence of his mother, he identified as a Catholic and professed respect for Church hierarchy and authority. From there he became a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lapsed_Catholic" title="Lapsed Catholic" id="mwAkE">non-practising Catholic</a> and increasingly expressed anti-Catholic and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-clerical" title="Anti-clerical" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAkI">anti-clerical</a> views. He frequented <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kardecist_spiritism" title="Kardecist spiritism" id="mwAkM">spiritism</a> during his exile (where he participated also in many <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Séance" title="Séance" id="mwAkQ">séances</a> conducted by Madame <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Delphine_de_Girardin" title="Delphine de Girardin" id="mwAkU">Delphine de Girardin</a>)<sup about="#mwt238" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-51" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-51"}}'><a href="#cite_note-51" id="mwAkY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAkc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkg">[</span>50<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkk">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt241" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-52" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-52"}}'><a href="#cite_note-52" id="mwAko"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAks"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAkw">[</span>51<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAk0">]</span></span></a></sup> and in later years settled into a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rationalist" title="Rationalist" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAk4">rationalist</a> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deism" title="Deism" id="mwAk8">deism</a> similar to that espoused by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voltaire" title="Voltaire" id="mwAlA">Voltaire</a>. A census-taker asked Hugo in 1872 if he was a Catholic, and he replied, "No. A <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_thought" title="Free thought" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAlE">Freethinker</a>."<sup about="#mwt244" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-53" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-53"}}'><a href="#cite_note-53" id="mwAlI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAlM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlQ">[</span>52<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlU">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAlY">After 1872, Hugo never lost his antipathy towards the Catholic Church. He felt the Church was indifferent to the plight of the working class under the oppression of the monarchy. Perhaps he also was upset by the frequency with which his work appeared on the Church's <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Index_Librorum_Prohibitorum" title="Index Librorum Prohibitorum" id="mwAlc">list of banned books</a>. Hugo counted 740 attacks on <i id="mwAlg">Les Misérables</i> in the Catholic press.<sup about="#mwt248" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-54" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-54"}}'><a href="#cite_note-54" id="mwAlk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAlo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAls">[</span>53<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAlw">]</span></span></a></sup> When Hugo's sons Charles and <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt245" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAl0" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"François-Victor"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;">François-Victor</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt245" id="mwAl4"/> died, he insisted that they be buried without a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crucifix" title="Crucifix" id="mwAl8">crucifix</a> or priest. In his will, he made the same stipulation about his own death and funeral.<sup about="#mwt251" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-55" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-55"}}'><a href="#cite_note-55" id="mwAmA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAmE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAmI">[</span>54<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAmM">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAmQ">Yet he believed in life after death and prayed every single morning and night, convinced as he wrote in <i id="mwAmU"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Man_Who_Laughs" title="The Man Who Laughs" id="mwAmY">The Man Who Laughs</a></i> that "Thanksgiving has wings and flies to its right destination. Your prayer knows its way better than you do."<sup about="#mwt254" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-56" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-56"}}'><a href="#cite_note-56" id="mwAmc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAmg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAmk">[</span>55<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAmo">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAms">Hugo's <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rationalism" title="Rationalism" id="mwAmw">rationalism</a> can be found in poems such as <i id="mwAm0"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torquemada_(play)" title="Torquemada (play)" id="mwAm4">Torquemada</a></i> (1869, about <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religious_fanaticism" title="Religious fanaticism" id="mwAm8">religious fanaticism</a>), <i id="mwAnA">The Pope</i> (1878, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-clerical" title="Anti-clerical" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAnE">anti-clerical</a>), <i id="mwAnI">Religions and Religion</i> (1880, denying the usefulness of churches) and, published posthumously, <i id="mwAnM">The End of Satan</i> and <i id="mwAnQ">God</i> (1886 and 1891 respectively, in which he represents Christianity as a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Griffin" title="Griffin" id="mwAnU">griffin</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rationalism" title="Rationalism" id="mwAnY">rationalism</a> as an <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angel" title="Angel" id="mwAnc">angel</a>).
<i id="mwAng"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hunchback_of_Notre-Dame" title="The Hunchback of Notre-Dame" id="mwAnk">The Hunchback of Notre-Dame</a></i> also garnered attention due to its portrayal of the abuse of power by the church, even getting listed as one of the "forbidden books" by it. In fact, earlier adaptations had to change the villain, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claude_Frollo" title="Claude Frollo" id="mwAno">Claude Frollo</a> from being a priest to avoid backlash.<sup about="#mwt257" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-57" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-57"}}'><a href="#cite_note-57" id="mwAns"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAnw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAn0">[</span>56<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAn4">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAn8"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vincent_van_Gogh" title="Vincent van Gogh" id="mwAoA">Vincent van Gogh</a> ascribed the saying "Religions pass away, but God remains," actually by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jules_Michelet" title="Jules Michelet" id="mwAoE">Jules Michelet</a>, to Hugo.<sup about="#mwt260" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-58" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-58"}}'><a href="#cite_note-58" id="mwAoI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAoM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAoQ">[</span>57<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAoU">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="5" id="mwAoY" aria-labelledby="Relationship_with_music"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Relationship_with_music">Relationship with music</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Victor_Hugo&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Relationship with music"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAoc">Although Hugo's many talents did not include exceptional musical ability, he nevertheless had a great impact on the music world through the inspiration that his works provided for composers of the 19th and 20th centuries. Hugo himself particularly enjoyed the music of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christoph_Willibald_Gluck" title="Christoph Willibald Gluck" id="mwAog">Gluck</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wolfgang_Amadeus_Mozart" title="Wolfgang Amadeus Mozart" id="mwAok">Mozart</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carl_Maria_von_Weber" title="Carl Maria von Weber" id="mwAoo">Weber</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giacomo_Meyerbeer" title="Giacomo Meyerbeer" id="mwAos">Meyerbeer</a>. In <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt261" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAow" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Les Misérables"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr">Les Misérables</i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt261" id="mwAo0"/>, he calls the huntsman's chorus in Weber's <i id="mwAo4"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euryanthe" title="Euryanthe" id="mwAo8">Euryanthe</a></i> "perhaps the most beautiful piece of music ever composed".<sup about="#mwt263" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-59" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-59"}}'><a href="#cite_note-59" id="mwApA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwApE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwApI">[</span>58<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwApM">]</span></span></a></sup> He also greatly admired <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ludwig_van_Beethoven" title="Ludwig van Beethoven" id="mwApQ">Beethoven</a> and, rather unusually for his time, appreciated works by composers from earlier centuries such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giovanni_Pierluigi_da_Palestrina" title="Giovanni Pierluigi da Palestrina" id="mwApU">Palestrina</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claudio_Monteverdi" title="Claudio Monteverdi" id="mwApY">Monteverdi</a>.<sup about="#mwt266" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-autogenerated2002_60-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"autogenerated2002"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-autogenerated2002-60"}}'><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated2002-60" id="mwApc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwApg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwApk">[</span>59<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwApo">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAps">Two famous musicians of the 19th century were friends of Hugo: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hector_Berlioz" title="Hector Berlioz" id="mwApw">Hector Berlioz</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franz_Liszt" title="Franz Liszt" id="mwAp0">Franz Liszt</a>. The latter played Beethoven in Hugo's home, and Hugo joked in a letter to a friend that, thanks to Liszt's piano lessons, he learned how to play a favourite song on the piano – with only one finger. </p>

<p id="mwAp4">Hugo also worked with composer <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louise_Bertin" title="Louise Bertin" id="mwAp8">Louise Bertin</a>, writing the libretto for her 1836 opera <i id="mwAqA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Esmeralda_(opera)" title="La Esmeralda (opera)" id="mwAqE">La Esmeralda</a></i>, which was based on the character in <i id="mwAqI">The Hunchback of Notre Dame</i>.<sup about="#mwt269" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-autogenerated2002_60-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"autogenerated2002"}}'><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated2002-60" id="mwAqM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAqQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqU">[</span>59<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqY">]</span></span></a></sup> Although for various reasons the opera closed soon after its fifth performance and is little known today, it has enjoyed a modern revival, both in a piano/song concert version by Liszt at the <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt267" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAqc" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Festival international Victor Hugo et Égaux"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;">Festival international Victor Hugo et Égaux</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt267" id="mwAqg"/> 2007<sup about="#mwt273" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-61" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-61"}}'><a href="#cite_note-61" id="mwAqk"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAqo"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqs">[</span>60<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAqw">]</span></span></a></sup> and in a full orchestral version presented in July 2008 at <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt268" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAq0" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Le Festival de Radio France et Montpellier Languedoc-Roussillon"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;">Le Festival de Radio France et Montpellier Languedoc-Roussillon</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt268" id="mwAq4"/>.<sup about="#mwt276" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-62" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-62"}}'><a href="#cite_note-62" id="mwAq8"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwArA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArE">[</span>61<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArI">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwArM">Hugo was also on friendly terms with <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frederic_Chopin" title="Frederic Chopin" class="mw-redirect" id="mwArQ">Frederic Chopin</a>. He introduced him to Amantine de Francueil (known as "<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Sand" title="George Sand" id="mwArU">George Sand</a>" despite being female) who became Chopin's lover. In 1849, Hugo attended Chopin's funeral. On the other hand, he had low esteem for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Wagner" title="Richard Wagner" id="mwArY">Richard Wagner</a>, whom he described as "a man of talent coupled with imbecility."<sup about="#mwt279" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-63" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-63"}}'><a href="#cite_note-63" id="mwArc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwArg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwArk">[</span>62<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAro">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwArs">Well over one thousand musical compositions have been inspired by Hugo's works from the 19th century until the present day. In particular, Hugo's plays, in which he rejected the rules of classical theatre in favour of romantic drama, attracted the interest of many composers who adapted them into operas. More than one hundred operas are based on Hugo's works and among them are <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaetano_Donizetti" title="Gaetano Donizetti" id="mwArw">Donizetti</a>'s <i id="mwAr0"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lucrezia_Borgia_(opera)" title="Lucrezia Borgia (opera)" id="mwAr4">Lucrezia Borgia</a></i> (1833), <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Giuseppe_Verdi" title="Giuseppe Verdi" id="mwAr8">Verdi</a>'s <span title="Italian-language text" about="#mwt280" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAsA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"it"},"2":{"wt":"[[Rigoletto]]"},"nocat":{"wt":"y"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="it"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rigoletto" title="Rigoletto">Rigoletto</a></i></span> (1851) and <i id="mwAsE"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernani" title="Ernani" id="mwAsI">Ernani</a></i> (1844), and <span title="Italian-language text" about="#mwt281" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAsM" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"it"},"2":{"wt":"[[Amilcare Ponchielli|Ponchielli]]"},"nocat":{"wt":"y"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="it"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amilcare_Ponchielli" title="Amilcare Ponchielli">Ponchielli</a></i></span><span typeof="mw:Nowiki" id="mwAsQ"></span>'s <span title="Italian-language text" about="#mwt282" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAsU" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"it"},"2":{"wt":"[[La Gioconda (opera)|La Gioconda]]"},"nocat":{"wt":"y"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="it"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Gioconda_(opera)" title="La Gioconda (opera)">La Gioconda</a></i></span> (1876).<sup about="#mwt287" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-autogenerated1981_64-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"autogenerated1981"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-autogenerated1981-64"}}'><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1981-64" id="mwAsY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAsc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsg">[</span>63<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAsk">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAso">Hugo's novels, as well as his plays, have been a great source of inspiration for musicians, stirring them to create not only opera and ballet but musical theatre such as <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt288" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAss" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Notre-Dame de Paris (musical)|Notre-Dame de Paris]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Notre-Dame_de_Paris_(musical)" title="Notre-Dame de Paris (musical)">Notre-Dame de Paris</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt288" id="mwAsw"/> and the ever-popular <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt289" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAs0" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Les Misérables (musical)|Les Misérables]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Les_Misérables_(musical)" title="Les Misérables (musical)">Les Misérables</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt289" id="mwAs4"/>, London's <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_End_of_London" title="West End of London" id="mwAs8">West End</a>'s longest running musical. Moreover, Hugo's poems have attracted an exceptional amount of interest from musicians, and numerous melodies have been based on his poetry by composers such as <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt290" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAtA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Berlioz"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;">Berlioz</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt290" id="mwAtE"/>, <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt291" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAtI" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Georges Bizet|Bizet]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georges_Bizet" title="Georges Bizet">Bizet</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt291" id="mwAtM"/>, <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt292" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAtQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Gabriel Fauré|Fauré]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gabriel_Fauré" title="Gabriel Fauré">Fauré</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt292" id="mwAtU"/>, <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt293" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAtY" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[César Franck|Franck]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/César_Franck" title="César Franck">Franck</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt293" id="mwAtc"/>, <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt294" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAtg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Édouard Lalo|Lalo]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Édouard_Lalo" title="Édouard Lalo">Lalo</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt294" id="mwAtk"/>, <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt295" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAto" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Liszt"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;">Liszt</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt295" id="mwAts"/>, <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt296" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAtw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Jules Massenet|Massenet]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jules_Massenet" title="Jules Massenet">Massenet</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt296" id="mwAt0"/>, <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt297" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAt4" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Camille Saint-Saëns|Saint-Saëns]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Camille_Saint-Saëns" title="Camille Saint-Saëns">Saint-Saëns</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt297" id="mwAt8"/>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sergei_Rachmaninoff" title="Sergei Rachmaninoff" id="mwAuA">Rachmaninoff</a>, and <span title="German-language text" about="#mwt298" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAuE" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"de"},"2":{"wt":"[[Richard Wagner|Wagner]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"},"nocat":{"wt":"y"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="de" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Wagner" title="Richard Wagner">Wagner</a></span></span>.<sup about="#mwt299" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-autogenerated1981_64-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"autogenerated1981"}}'><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1981-64" id="mwAuI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAuM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAuQ">[</span>63<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAuU">]</span></span></a></sup> Composers <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugenie_de_Santa_Coloma_Sourget" title="Eugenie de Santa Coloma Sourget" id="mwAuY">Eugenie Santa Coloma Sourget</a><sup about="#mwt302" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-65" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-65"}}'><a href="#cite_note-65" id="mwAuc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAug"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAuk">[</span>64<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAuo">]</span></span></a></sup> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dagmar_Rybner" title="Dagmar Rybner" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAus">Dagmar Rybner</a><sup about="#mwt305" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-66" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-66"}}'><a href="#cite_note-66" id="mwAuw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAu0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAu4">[</span>65<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAu8">]</span></span></a></sup> also used Hugo’s texts in their songs.</p>

<p id="mwAvA">Today, Hugo's work continues to stimulate musicians to create new compositions. For example, Hugo's novel against capital punishment, <i id="mwAvE">The Last Day of a Condemned Man</i>, was adapted into an opera by <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt306" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAvI" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[David Alagna]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_Alagna" title="David Alagna">David Alagna</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt306" id="mwAvM"/>, with a libretto by <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt307" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAvQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Frédérico Alagna"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;">Frédérico Alagna</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt307" id="mwAvU"/> and premièred by their brother, tenor <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt308" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAvY" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Roberto Alagna]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roberto_Alagna" title="Roberto Alagna">Roberto Alagna</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt308" id="mwAvc"/>, in 2007.<sup about="#mwt315" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-67" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-67"}}'><a href="#cite_note-67" id="mwAvg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAvk"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAvo">[</span>66<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAvs">]</span></span></a></sup> In Guernsey, every two years, the Victor Hugo International Music Festival attracts a wide range of musicians and the premiere of songs specially commissioned from such composers as <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt309" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAvw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Guillaume Connesson]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guillaume_Connesson" title="Guillaume Connesson">Guillaume Connesson</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt309" id="mwAv0"/>, <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt310" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAv4" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"{{ill|Richard Dubugnon|fr}}"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Dubugnon?action=edit&amp;redlink=1" title="Richard Dubugnon (page does not exist)" class="new" typeof="mw:LocalizedAttrs" data-mw-i18n='{"title":{"lang":"x-page","key":"red-link-title","params":["Richard Dubugnon"]}}'>Richard Dubugnon</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal; "><span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><a rel="mw:WikiLink/Interwiki" href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard%20Dubugnon" title="fr:Richard Dubugnon" class="extiw">fr</a><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></span></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt310" id="mwAv8"/>, <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt311" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAwA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Olivier Kaspar]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olivier_Kaspar?action=edit&amp;redlink=1" title="Olivier Kaspar (page does not exist)" class="new" typeof="mw:LocalizedAttrs" data-mw-i18n='{"title":{"lang":"x-page","key":"red-link-title","params":["Olivier Kaspar"]}}'>Olivier Kaspar</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt311" id="mwAwE"/>, and <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt312" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAwI" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Thierry Escaich]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thierry_Escaich" title="Thierry Escaich">Thierry Escaich</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt312" id="mwAwM"/> and based on Hugo's poetry.</p>

<p id="mwAwQ">Remarkably, not only has Hugo's literary production been the source of inspiration for musical works, but also his political writings have received attention from musicians and have been adapted to music. For instance, in 2009, Italian composer Matteo Sommacal was commissioned by Festival "Bagliori d'autore" and wrote a piece for speaker and chamber ensemble entitled <i id="mwAwU">Actes et paroles</i>, with a text elaborated by Chiara Piola Caselli after Victor Hugo's last political speech addressed to the Assemblée législative, "Sur la Revision de la Constitution" (18 July 1851),<sup about="#mwt317" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-68" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-68"}}'><a href="#cite_note-68" id="mwAwY"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAwc"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAwg">[</span>67<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAwk">]</span></span></a></sup> and premiered in Rome on 19 November 2009, in the auditorium of the Institut français, Centre Saint-Louis, French Embassy to the Holy See, by Piccola Accademia degli Specchi featuring the composer <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthias_Kadar?action=edit&amp;redlink=1" title="Matthias Kadar (page does not exist)" about="#mwt316" typeof="mw:Transclusion mw:LocalizedAttrs" class="new" id="mwAwo" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"ill","href":"./Template:Ill"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Matthias Kadar"},"2":{"wt":"nl"}},"i":0}}]}' data-mw-i18n='{"title":{"lang":"x-page","key":"red-link-title","params":["Matthias Kadar"]}}'>Matthias Kadar</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal; " about="#mwt316" id="mwAws"><span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><a rel="mw:WikiLink/Interwiki" href="https://nl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthias%20Kadar" title="nl:Matthias Kadar" class="extiw">nl</a><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></span>.<sup about="#mwt318" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-69" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-69"}}'><a href="#cite_note-69" id="mwAww"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAw0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAw4">[</span>68<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAw8">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="6" id="mwAxA" aria-labelledby="Declining_years_and_death"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Declining_years_and_death">Declining years and death</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Victor_Hugo&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Declining years and death"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwAxE"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Victor_Hugo_Vanity_Fair_20_September_1879.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwAxI"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Victor_Hugo_Vanity_Fair_20_September_1879.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/76/Victor_Hugo_Vanity_Fair_20_September_1879.jpg/250px-Victor_Hugo_Vanity_Fair_20_September_1879.jpg" decoding="async" data-file-width="877" data-file-height="1525" data-file-type="bitmap" height="330" width="190" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/76/Victor_Hugo_Vanity_Fair_20_September_1879.jpg/500px-Victor_Hugo_Vanity_Fair_20_September_1879.jpg 2x" class="mw-file-element mw-file-upright" style="--mw-file-upright: 0.75" id="mwAxM"/></a><figcaption id="mwAxQ">Caricature of Hugo in the London magazine <i id="mwAxU"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vanity_Fair_(British_magazine)" title="Vanity Fair (British magazine)" id="mwAxY">Vanity Fair</a></i>, September 1879</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwAxc"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Cortège_funèbre_de_Victor_Hugo_arrivant_au_Panthéon.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwAxg"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Cortège_funèbre_de_Victor_Hugo_arrivant_au_Panthéon.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Cort%C3%A8ge_fun%C3%A8bre_de_Victor_Hugo_arrivant_au_Panth%C3%A9on.jpg/250px-Cort%C3%A8ge_fun%C3%A8bre_de_Victor_Hugo_arrivant_au_Panth%C3%A9on.jpg" decoding="async" data-file-width="1200" data-file-height="1600" data-file-type="bitmap" height="333" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b5/Cort%C3%A8ge_fun%C3%A8bre_de_Victor_Hugo_arrivant_au_Panth%C3%A9on.jpg/500px-Cort%C3%A8ge_fun%C3%A8bre_de_Victor_Hugo_arrivant_au_Panth%C3%A9on.jpg 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwAxk"/></a><figcaption id="mwAxo">Funeral procession of Victor Hugo arriving at the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panthéon" title="Panthéon" id="mwAxs">Panthéon</a></figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwAxw">When Hugo returned to Paris in 1870, the country hailed him as a national hero. He was confident that he would be offered the dictatorship, as shown by the notes he kept at the time: "Dictatorship is a crime. This is a crime I am going to commit," but he felt he had to assume that responsibility.<sup about="#mwt319" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-70" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-70"}}'><a href="#cite_note-70" id="mwAx0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAx4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAx8">[</span>69<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAyA">]</span></span></a></sup> Despite his popularity, Hugo lost his bid for re-election to the National Assembly in 1872.</p>

<p id="mwAyE">Throughout his life Hugo kept believing in unstoppable humanistic progress. In his last public address on 3 August 1879 he prophesied, in an over-optimistic way, "In the twentieth century war will be dead, the scaffold will be dead, hatred will be dead, frontier boundaries will be dead, dogmas will be dead; man will live."<sup about="#mwt322" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-71" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-71"}}'><a href="#cite_note-71" id="mwAyI"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAyM"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAyQ">[</span>70<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAyU">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAyY">Within a brief period, he suffered a mild stroke, his daughter Adèle was interned in an <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insane_asylum" title="Insane asylum" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAyc">insane asylum</a>, and his two sons died. (Adèle's biography inspired the movie <i id="mwAyg"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Story_of_Adele_H." title="The Story of Adele H." class="mw-redirect" id="mwAyk">The Story of Adele H.</a></i>) His wife Adèle had died in 1868.
His faithful mistress <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juliette_Drouet" title="Juliette Drouet" id="mwAyo">Juliette Drouet</a> died in 1883, only two years before his own death. </p>
<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwAys"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Victor_Hugo_deathbed_portrait_by_Nadar,_1885.png" class="mw-file-description" id="mwAyw"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Victor_Hugo_deathbed_portrait_by_Nadar,_1885.png" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Victor_Hugo_deathbed_portrait_by_Nadar%2C_1885.png/250px-Victor_Hugo_deathbed_portrait_by_Nadar%2C_1885.png" decoding="async" data-file-width="1987" data-file-height="1515" data-file-type="bitmap" height="153" width="201" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Victor_Hugo_deathbed_portrait_by_Nadar%2C_1885.png/500px-Victor_Hugo_deathbed_portrait_by_Nadar%2C_1885.png 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwAy0"/></a><figcaption id="mwAy4">Hugo on his deathbed (photographed by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nadar" title="Nadar" id="mwAy8">Nadar</a>)</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwAzA"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Panthéon_Victor_Hugo.JPG" class="mw-file-description" id="mwAzE"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Panthéon_Victor_Hugo.JPG" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Panth%C3%A9on_Victor_Hugo.JPG/250px-Panth%C3%A9on_Victor_Hugo.JPG" decoding="async" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1536" data-file-type="bitmap" height="150" width="200" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/24/Panth%C3%A9on_Victor_Hugo.JPG/500px-Panth%C3%A9on_Victor_Hugo.JPG 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwAzI"/></a><figcaption id="mwAzM">Tomb of Victor Hugo at the <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt323" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAzQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Panthéon]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panthéon" title="Panthéon">Panthéon</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt323" id="mwAzU"/></figcaption></figure>

<p id="mwAzY">Despite his personal loss, Hugo remained committed to the cause of political change. On 30 January 1876, he was elected to the newly created Senate. This last phase of his political career was considered a failure. Hugo was a maverick and achieved little in the Senate. He suffered a mild stroke on 27 June 1878.<sup about="#mwt325" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-72" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-72"}}'><a href="#cite_note-72" id="mwAzc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAzg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAzk">[</span>71<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAzo">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt328" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-bandw_73-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"bandw"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-bandw-73"}}'><a href="#cite_note-bandw-73" id="mwAzs"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAzw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAz0">[</span>72<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAz4">]</span></span></a></sup> To honour the fact that he was entering his 80th year, he received one of the greatest tributes to a living writer ever held. The celebrations began on 25 June 1881, when Hugo was presented with a <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt324" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAz8" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Manufacture nationale de Sèvres|Sèvres]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manufacture_nationale_de_Sèvres" title="Manufacture nationale de Sèvres">Sèvres</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt324" id="mwA0A"/> vase, the traditional gift for sovereigns. On 27 June, one of the largest parades in French history was held.</p>

<p id="mwA0E">Marchers stretched from the <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt329" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwA0I" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Avenue d&apos;Eylau"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;">Avenue d'Eylau</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt329" id="mwA0M"/>, where the author was living, down the <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt330" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwA0Q" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Champs-Élysées]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Champs-Élysées" title="Champs-Élysées">Champs-Élysées</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt330" id="mwA0U"/>, and all the way to the centre of Paris. The paraders marched for six hours past Hugo as he sat at the window at his house. Every inch and detail of the event was for Hugo; the official guides even wore cornflowers as an allusion to Fantine's song in <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt331" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwA0Y" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Les Misérables"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr">Les Misérables</i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt331" id="mwA0c"/>. On 28 June, the city of Paris changed the name of the Avenue d'Eylau to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avenue_Victor-Hugo_(Paris)" title="Avenue Victor-Hugo (Paris)" id="mwA0g">Avenue Victor-Hugo</a>.<sup about="#mwt334" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-74" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-74"}}'><a href="#cite_note-74" id="mwA0k"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA0o"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0s">[</span>73<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA0w">]</span></span></a></sup> Letters addressed to the author were from then on labelled "To Mister Victor Hugo, In his avenue, Paris". Two days before dying, he left a note with these last words: "To love is to act."</p>

<p id="mwA00">On 20 May 1885, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Petit_Journal_(newspaper)" title="Le Petit Journal (newspaper)" id="mwA04"><i id="mwA08">le Petit Journal</i></a> published the official medical bulletin on Hugo's health condition. "The illustrious patient" was fully conscious and aware that there was no hope for him. They also reported from a reliable source that at one point in the night he had whispered the following <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt335" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwA1A" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[alexandrin]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandrin" title="Alexandrin" class="mw-redirect">alexandrin</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt335" id="mwA1E"/>, "En moi c’est le combat du jour et de la nuit."—"In me, this is the battle between day and night."<sup about="#mwt338" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-75" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-75"}}'><a href="#cite_note-75" id="mwA1I"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA1M"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA1Q">[</span>74<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA1U">]</span></span></a></sup> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Le_Matin_(France)" title="Le Matin (France)" id="mwA1Y"><i id="mwA1c">Le Matin</i></a> published a slightly different version: "Here is the battle between day and night."</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwA1g"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Vue_du_catafalque_sous_l'Arc_de_Triomphe_depuis_l'avenue_de_la_Grande_Armée.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwA1k"><img alt="" resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Vue_du_catafalque_sous_l'Arc_de_Triomphe_depuis_l'avenue_de_la_Grande_Armée.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/Vue_du_catafalque_sous_l%27Arc_de_Triomphe_depuis_l%27avenue_de_la_Grande_Arm%C3%A9e.jpg/250px-Vue_du_catafalque_sous_l%27Arc_de_Triomphe_depuis_l%27avenue_de_la_Grande_Arm%C3%A9e.jpg" decoding="async" data-file-width="4330" data-file-height="5160" data-file-type="bitmap" height="298" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/Vue_du_catafalque_sous_l%27Arc_de_Triomphe_depuis_l%27avenue_de_la_Grande_Arm%C3%A9e.jpg/500px-Vue_du_catafalque_sous_l%27Arc_de_Triomphe_depuis_l%27avenue_de_la_Grande_Arm%C3%A9e.jpg 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwA1o"/></a><figcaption id="mwA1s">The <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catafalque" title="Catafalque" id="mwA1w">Catafalque</a> of Victor Hugo under the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arc_de_Triomphe" title="Arc de Triomphe" id="mwA10">Arc de Triomphe</a> on 1 June 1885</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwA14">Hugo died on 22 May 1885, at 50 Avenue Victor Hugo (now number 124). His death, from pneumonia, generated intense national mourning. He was not only revered as a towering figure in literature, he was a statesman who shaped the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Third_Republic" title="French Third Republic" id="mwA18">Third Republic</a> and democracy in France. All his life he remained a defender of liberty, equality and fraternity as well as an adamant champion of French culture. In 1877, aged 75, he wrote, "I am not one of these sweet-tempered old men. I am still exasperated and violent. I shout and I feel indignant and I cry. Woe to anyone who harms France! I do declare I will die a fanatic patriot."<sup about="#mwt341" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-76" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-76"}}'><a href="#cite_note-76" id="mwA2A"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA2E"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA2I">[</span>75<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA2M">]</span></span></a></sup></p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwA2Q"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Funérailles_de_Victor_Hugo_-_Foule_et_personnalités_devant_la_colonnade_du_Panthéon.jpg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwA2U"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Funérailles_de_Victor_Hugo_-_Foule_et_personnalités_devant_la_colonnade_du_Panthéon.jpg" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/63/Fun%C3%A9railles_de_Victor_Hugo_-_Foule_et_personnalit%C3%A9s_devant_la_colonnade_du_Panth%C3%A9on.jpg/250px-Fun%C3%A9railles_de_Victor_Hugo_-_Foule_et_personnalit%C3%A9s_devant_la_colonnade_du_Panth%C3%A9on.jpg" decoding="async" data-file-width="5079" data-file-height="4195" data-file-type="bitmap" height="206" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/63/Fun%C3%A9railles_de_Victor_Hugo_-_Foule_et_personnalit%C3%A9s_devant_la_colonnade_du_Panth%C3%A9on.jpg/500px-Fun%C3%A9railles_de_Victor_Hugo_-_Foule_et_personnalit%C3%A9s_devant_la_colonnade_du_Panth%C3%A9on.jpg 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwA2Y"/></a><figcaption id="mwA2c">Crowd of mourners at the funeral,<br id="mwA2g"/>1 June 1885.</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwA2k">Although he had requested a pauper's funeral, by decree of President <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jules_Grévy" title="Jules Grévy" id="mwA2o">Jules Grévy</a> he was awarded a state funeral, an event described by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nietzsche" title="Nietzsche" class="mw-redirect" id="mwA2s">Nietzsche</a> as a "veritable orgy of bad taste".<sup about="#mwt349" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-77" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-77"}}'><a href="#cite_note-77" id="mwA2w"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA20"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA24">[</span>76<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA28">]</span></span></a></sup> More than two million people joined Hugo's funeral procession in Paris from the <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt342" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwA3A" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Arc de Triomphe]]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i lang="fr"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arc_de_Triomphe" title="Arc de Triomphe">Arc de Triomphe</a></i></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt342" id="mwA3E"/> to the <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt343" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwA3I" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Panthéon]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panthéon" title="Panthéon">Panthéon</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt343" id="mwA3M"/>, where he was buried. He shares a crypt within the <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt344" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwA3Q" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"Panthéon"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;">Panthéon</span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt344" id="mwA3U"/> with <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt345" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwA3Y" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Alexandre Dumas]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexandre_Dumas" title="Alexandre Dumas">Alexandre Dumas</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt345" id="mwA3c"/> and <span title="French-language text" about="#mwt346" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwA3g" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"lang","href":"./Template:Lang"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"fr"},"2":{"wt":"[[Émile Zola]]"},"italic":{"wt":"no"}},"i":0}}]}'><span lang="fr" style="font-style: normal;"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Émile_Zola" title="Émile Zola">Émile Zola</a></span></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_containing_French-language_text" about="#mwt346" id="mwA3k"/>. Most large French towns and cities have a street or square named after him.</p>

<p id="mwA3o">Hugo left five sentences as his last will, to be officially published:</p>

<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1244412712" about="#mwt350" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" id="mwA3s" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Template:Blockquote/styles.css"},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"blockquote","href":"./Template:Blockquote"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&lt;poem lang=\"fr\" style=\"margin-center: 1em; font-style: italic;\">\nJe donne cinquante mille francs aux pauvres.
